# DTC P0443 (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY)

DTC P0443 : EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction Is DTC P0443 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the EVAP canister purge valve and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

Is DTC P0443 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the EVAP canister purge valve and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (EVAP canister purge valve stuck open failure, EVAP canister purge valve stuck closed failure) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 131 deg.F (55 deg.C). -2. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected. -3. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the EVAP canister purge valve as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Start the engine, and let it idle. Is there vacuum? YES The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck open. Go to step 3. NO The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck closed. Go to step 5.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 131 deg.F (55 deg.C).

-2. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected.

-3. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the EVAP canister purge valve as shown.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Start the engine, and let it idle.

Is there vacuum?

YES

The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck open. Go to step 3.

NO

The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck closed. Go to step 5.

- Determine possible failure area (IPV line, EVAP canister purge valve) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector: disconnected Test point 1 EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector

-3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode

EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Test point 2 | Body ground

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

- Shorted wire check (IPV line) -1. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -2. Disconnect the following connector. PCM connector E (80P) -3.
````

# DTC P0450 (K20C1) (2020 2021)

DTC P0450 : FTP Sensor Out of Range

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check 1 -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor More than 6 kPa Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

- Duplicate sources: `pages\9062.html`, `pages\22297.html`, `pages\15197.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0450 (K20C2) (19-21)

DTC P0450 : FTP Sensor Out of Range

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor More than 3.62 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck .

NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box.

-5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | More than 3.62 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck. -2. Start the engine. -3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0450 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck.

-2. Start the engine.

-3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0450 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Determine possible failure area (FTP sensor, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. FTP sensor 3P connector -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Terminal B FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor More than 3.62 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the FTP sensor .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

FTP sensor 3P connector

-3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire.

Terminal A | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Terminal B | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | More than 3.62 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Replace the FTP sensor .

- Determine possible failure area (SG6 line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove the jumper wire from the FTP sensor 3P connector. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

# DTC P0450 (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY) (19-21)

DTC P0450 : FTP Sensor Out of Range

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor More than 3.62 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck .

NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box.

-5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | More than 3.62 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck. -2. Start the engine. -3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0450 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck.

-2. Start the engine.

-3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0450 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0450 FTP Sensor Out of Range

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Determine possible failure area (FTP sensor, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. FTP sensor 3P connector -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Terminal B FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor More than 3.62 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the FTP sensor .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

FTP sensor 3P connector

-3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire.

Terminal A | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Terminal B | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | More than 3.62 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Replace the FTP sensor .

- Determine possible failure area (SG6 line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove the jumper wire from the FTP sensor 3P connector. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

# DTC P0451 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0451 : FTP Sensor Circuit Range/Performance Problem

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If DTC P2422 is stored at the same time as DTC P0451, troubleshoot DTC P2422 first, then recheck for DTC P0451.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0451 FTP Sensor Circuit Range/Performance Problem

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes or more in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: Engine Speed Vehicle Speed Baro Sensor IAT Sensor (1) On-board Snapshot Signal Current conditions Values Unit Engine Speed Vehicle Speed Baro Sensor IAT Sensor (1) -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0451 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0451 FTP Sensor Circuit Range/Performance Problem Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Replace the FTP sensor . NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

- Engine Speed

- Vehicle Speed

- Baro Sensor

- IAT Sensor (1)

Values | Unit

Engine Speed

IAT Sensor (1)

P0451 FTP Sensor Circuit Range/Performance Problem

YES

The failure is duplicated. Replace the FTP sensor .

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

## Chunk 6665: DTC P0452 (K20C2) (18-21)

- Title: DTC P0452 (K20C2) (18-21)
- Source path: `pages\7482.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_b870e5a63254`
- Images: `images\GHH404806.png`, `images\GHH404807.jpeg`, `images\GHH404808.png`, `images\GHH404809.jpeg`, `images\GHH404810.png`, `images\GHH404811.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\9069.html`, `pages\22304.html`, `pages\15202.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0452 (K20C2) (18-21)

DTC P0452 : FTP Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0452 FTP Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor Less than -57.21 mmHg Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck .

NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box.

-5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | Less than -57.21 | mmHg

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck. -2. Start the engine, and let it idle. -3. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0452 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0452 FTP Sensor Circuit Low Voltage Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck.

-2. Start the engine, and let it idle.

-3. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0452 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0452 FTP Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Determine possible failure area (PTANK line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. FTP sensor 3P connector -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP Sensor Less than -57.21 mmHg Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Go to step 5.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

FTP sensor 3P connector

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP Sensor | Less than -57.21 | mmHg

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Go to step 5.

- Shorted wire check (PTANK line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the following connector. PCM connector A (50P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode FTP sensor 3P connector: disconnected PCM connector A (50P): disconnected Test point 1 FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Repair a short in the PTANK wire between the PCM (A42) and the FTP sensor. NO The PTANK wire is OK. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC P0452 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2.

# DTC P0453 (K20C1) (17-19)

DTC P0453 : FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP SENSOR More than 4.84 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck .

NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box.

-5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-6. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP SENSOR | More than 4.84 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck. -2. Start the engine. -3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0453 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck.

-2. Start the engine.

-3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0453 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Determine possible failure area (FTP sensor, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. FTP sensor 3P connector -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Terminal B FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP SENSOR More than 4.84 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the FTP sensor .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

FTP sensor 3P connector

-3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire.

Terminal A | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Terminal B | FTP sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

# DTC P0453 (K20C2) (16-18)

DTC P0453 : FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP SENSOR More than 4.7 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck .

NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

FTP SENSOR | More than 4.7 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck. -2. Start the engine. -3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0453 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Remove the emergency fuel funnel from the fuel filler neck.

-2. Start the engine.

-3. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0453 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A FTP sensor 3P connector No. 1 Terminal B FTP sensor 3P connector No. 2 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP SENSOR More than 4.7 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the FTP sensor .

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

FTP sensor 3P connector

-3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire.

Terminal A | FTP sensor 3P connector No. 1

Terminal B | FTP sensor 3P connector No. 2

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

YES

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC (PGM-FI)

- EVAP system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Insert the emergency fuel funnel into the fuel filler neck . NOTE: The emergency fuel funnel is stored in the trunk tool box. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit FTP SENSOR More than 4.7 V Do the current condition (s) match the malfunction threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

YES

Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0453 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0453 FTP Sensor Circuit High Voltage Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. ````text
# DTC P0455, P0456 (K20C2)

DTC P0455 : EVAP System Large Leak Detected

DTC P0456 : EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

Vacuum/Pressure Gauge, 0-4 inHg 07JAZ-001000B

NOTICE:

The fuel system is designed to allow specified maximum vacuum and pressure conditions. Do not deviate from the vacuum and pressure tests as indicated in these procedures. Excessive pressure/vacuum would damage the EVAP components or cause eventual fuel tank failure.

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Fresh fuel has a higher volatility that will create greater pressure/vacuum. The best condition for testing is less than a full tank of fresh fuel. If possible, to assist in leak detection, add 1 gallon of fresh fuel to the tank (as long as it will not fill the tank), just before starting these procedures.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0455 EVAP System Large Leak Detected

P0456 EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS. FUNCTION TEST Is the result OK? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor, the EVAP canister purge valve, the EVAP canister vent shut valve, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the vehicle returns with the same DTC, continue with step 2. NO The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS.

FUNCTION TEST

Is the result OK?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor, the EVAP canister purge valve, the EVAP canister vent shut valve, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the vehicle returns with the same DTC, continue with step 2.

NO

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

- EVAP canister vent hose check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove the right front floor undercover . -3. Check the EVAP canister vent hose (A) for a blockage or a restriction. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there a blockage or a restriction in the hose? YES Remove a blockage or a restriction, if needed, replace the EVAP canister vent hose. NO Go to step 3.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Remove the right front floor undercover .

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there a blockage or a restriction in the hose?

YES

Remove a blockage or a restriction, if needed, replace the EVAP canister vent hose.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Fuel tank vapor recirculation tube visual check -1. Check for a poor connection or damage at the fuel tank vapor recirculation tube. Is the tube OK? YES The tube is OK. Go to step 4. NO Replace the fuel tank .

-1. Check for a poor connection or damage at the fuel tank vapor recirculation tube.

Is the tube OK?

YES

The tube is OK. Go to step 4.

NO

- EVAP canister purge valve leak check -1. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -2. Slowly apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the hose. Does it hold vacuum for 1 minute? YES Leak not detected at the EVAP canister purge valve. Go to step 5. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0455, P0456 (L15B7)

DTC P0455 : EVAP System Large Leak Detected

DTC P0456 : EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

Vacuum/Pressure Gauge, 0-4 inHg 07JAZ-001000B

NOTICE:

The fuel system is designed to allow specified maximum vacuum and pressure conditions. Do not deviate from the vacuum and pressure tests as indicated in these procedures. Excessive pressure/vacuum would damage the EVAP components or cause eventual fuel tank failure.

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Fresh fuel has a higher volatility that will create greater pressure/vacuum. The best condition for testing is less than a full tank of fresh fuel. If possible, to assist in leak detection, add 1 gallon of fresh fuel to the tank (as long as it will not fill the tank), just before starting these procedures.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0455 EVAP System Large Leak Detected

P0456 EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS. FUNCTION TEST Is the result OK? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor, the EVAP canister purge valve, the EVAP canister vent shut valve, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the vehicle returns with the same DTC, continue with step 4. NO The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS.

FUNCTION TEST

YES

If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the vehicle returns with the same DTC, continue with step 4.

NO

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

- EVAP canister vent hose check -1. Remove the right front floor undercover . -2. Check the EVAP canister vent hose (A) for a blockage or a restriction. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there a blockage or a restriction in the hose? YES Remove a blockage or a restriction, if needed, replace the EVAP canister vent hose. NO Go to step 3.

-2. Check the EVAP canister vent hose (A) for a blockage or a restriction.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

YES

Remove a blockage or a restriction, if needed, replace the EVAP canister vent hose.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Fuel tank vapor recirculation tube visual check -1. Check for a poor connection or damage at the fuel tank vapor recirculation tube. Is the tube OK? YES The tube is OK. Go to step 4. NO Replace the fuel tank or replace the fuel tank unit if needed.

-1. Check for a poor connection or damage at the fuel tank vapor recirculation tube.

Is the tube OK?

YES

The tube is OK. Go to step 4.

NO

Replace the fuel tank or replace the fuel tank unit if needed.

- EVAP canister purge valve leak check -1. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected. -2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -3. Slowly apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the hose. Does it hold vacuum for 1 minute? YES Leak not detected at the EVAP canister purge valve. Go to step 5. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-3.

# DTC P0460 (K20C2) (19-21)

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

# DTC P0461 (K20C1) (17-19)

DTC P0461 : Fuel Level Sensor (Fuel Gauge Sending Unit) Circuit Range/Performance Problem

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0461 Fuel Level Sensor (Fuel Gauge Sending Unit) Circuit Range/Performance Problem

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Fuel gauge sending unit performance check -1. Test the fuel gauge sending unit . Is the fuel gauge sending unit OK? YES Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the fuel gauge sending unit and the gauge control module. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . NO Replace the fuel gauge sending unit .

-1. Test the fuel gauge sending unit .

Is the fuel gauge sending unit OK?

YES

Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the fuel gauge sending unit and the gauge control module. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

NO

Replace the fuel gauge sending unit .
````

# DTC P0461 (K20C1) (2020 2021)

DTC P0461 : Fuel Level Sensor (Fuel Gauge Sending Unit) Circuit Range/Performance Problem

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Because it requires 124 miles (200 km) of driving without refueling to complete this diagnosis, DTC P0461 cannot be duplicated during this troubleshooting.

- DTC P0461 may be stored due to the following:

- - Repeated small amounts of refueling - Fuel draining during repairs - Constant cornering or driving on a sloping road for a certain time

- - Repeated small amounts of refueling

Repeated small amounts of refueling

- - Fuel draining during repairs

Fuel draining during repairs

- - Constant cornering or driving on a sloping road for a certain time

Constant cornering or driving on a sloping road for a certain time

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0461 Fuel Level Sensor (Fuel Gauge Sending Unit) Circuit Range/Performance Problem

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Customer usage check -1. Ask the customer about their refueling method. Has the vehicle been repeatedly refilled with less than 1.32 US gal (5.0 L) at each refuel? YES Direct the customer on the proper refilling conditions in the How to Refuel section of the owner's manual. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Ask the customer about their refueling method.

Has the vehicle been repeatedly refilled with less than 1.32 US gal (5.0 L) at each refuel?

YES

Direct the customer on the proper refilling conditions in the How to Refuel section of the owner's manual.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Fuel gauge sending unit performance check -1. Test the fuel gauge sending unit . Is the fuel gauge sending unit OK? YES Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the fuel gauge sending unit and the gauge control module. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . NO Replace the fuel gauge sending unit .

-1. Test the fuel gauge sending unit .

Is the fuel gauge sending unit OK?

YES

Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the fuel gauge sending unit and the gauge control module. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

NO

Replace the fuel gauge sending unit .
````

# DTC P0480 (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY)

DTC P0480 : RFC System Malfunction

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0480 RFC System Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Select the RADIATOR FAN in the INSPECTION MENU with the HDS. RADIATOR FAN Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0480 RFC System Malfunction Is DTC P0480 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the RFC unit, the RFC relay, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

# DTC P0496 (K20C1) (2017 2018)

DTC P0496 : EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

Vacuum/Pressure Gauge, 0-4 inHg 07JAZ-001000B

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0496 EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS. NOTE: If the test could not be completed because of another DTC, do the troubleshooting for that DTC. FUNCTION TEST Is the result OK? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check the EVAP canister purge valve for debris that may cause it to stick open intermittently. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . NO The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS.

NOTE: If the test could not be completed because of another DTC, do the troubleshooting for that DTC.

FUNCTION TEST

Is the result OK?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check the EVAP canister purge valve for debris that may cause it to stick open intermittently. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

NO

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

- EVAP canister purge valve open stuck check -1. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected. -2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -3. Apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the EVAP canister purge valve. Does it hold vacuum? YES Go to step 3. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-1. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected.

-2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-3. Apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the EVAP canister purge valve.

Does it hold vacuum?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

- Non-return valve check -1. Inspect the non-return valve . Is the valve OK? YES Replace the EVAP canister vent shut valve . NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-1. Inspect the non-return valve .

Is the valve OK?

YES

Replace the EVAP canister vent shut valve .

NO

Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .
````

# DTC P0498 (K20C2)

DTC P0498 : EVAP Canister Vent Shut Valve Circuit Low Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0498 EVAP Canister Vent Shut Valve Circuit Low Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification 1 -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) for at least 5 seconds. -4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0498 EVAP Canister Vent Shut Valve Circuit Low Voltage Is DTC P0498 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO Go to step 2.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) for at least 5 seconds.

-4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0498 EVAP Canister Vent Shut Valve Circuit Low Voltage

Is DTC P0498 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification 2 -1. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, and do the EVAP CVS ON in the SINGLE SOLENOID with the HDS. SINGLE SOLENOID Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0498 EVAP Canister Vent Shut Valve Circuit Low Voltage Is DTC P0498 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the EVAP canister vent shut valve and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. NO Check the No. A11 (5 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box, and repair a short in the No. A11 (5 A) fuse circuit if needed. Also replace the No. A11 (5 A) fuse. If the No. A11 (5 A) fuse is OK, repair an open in the IGPS (LAF) wire between the No. A11 (5 A) fuse and the EVAP canister vent shut valve.

Test point 1 | PCM connector A (50P) No. 6

Test point 2 | Body ground

Is there continuity?

YES

The VSV wire is OK. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck.
````

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P04DF (K20C1) (19-21)

DTC P04DF : EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

Vacuum/Pressure Gauge, 0-4 inHg 07JAZ-001000B

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P04DF EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS. NOTE: If the test could not be completed because of another DTC, do the troubleshooting for that DTC. FUNCTION TEST Is the result OK? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check the EVAP canister purge valve for debris that may cause it to stick open intermittently. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . NO The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Select the EVAP TEST in the INSPECTION MENU, then select the FUNCTION TEST with the HDS.

NOTE: If the test could not be completed because of another DTC, do the troubleshooting for that DTC.

FUNCTION TEST

Is the result OK?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check the EVAP canister purge valve for debris that may cause it to stick open intermittently. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

NO

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

- EVAP canister purge valve open stuck check -1. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected. -2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -3. Apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the EVAP canister purge valve. Does it hold vacuum? YES Go to step 3. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-1. Remove the EVAP canister purge valve with its connector and vacuum hose connected.

-2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a T-fitting (C), a vacuum gauge, and a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the vacuum hose as shown.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-3. Apply about 2 kPa (0.6 inHg, 15 mmHg) of vacuum to the EVAP canister purge valve.

Does it hold vacuum?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

- Non-return valve check -1. Inspect the non-return valve . Is the valve OK? YES Replace the EVAP canister vent shut valve . NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-1. Inspect the non-return valve .

Is the valve OK?

YES

Replace the EVAP canister vent shut valve .

NO

Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .
````

# DTC P0500, P2158 (K20C1) (20-21)

DTC P0500 : Vehicle Speed Sensor A No Signal

DTC P2158 : Vehicle Speed Sensor B No Signal

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0500 Vehicle Speed Sensor A No Signal

P2158 Vehicle Speed Sensor B No Signal

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Clear the VSA system DTCs with the HDS. Clear DTC (VSA) -4. Start the engine. Test-drive the vehicle under these conditions: Vehicle speed above 19 mph (30 km/h) for more than 30 seconds Signal Current conditions Values Unit Vehicle Speed -6. Check the following VSA system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0030-38 Magnetic Encoder (Left Front) Failure (Pulse Missing) C0031-14 Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) C0031-62 Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure C0033-38 Magnetic Encoder (Right Front) Failure (Pulse Missing) DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0034-14 Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Circuit Failure (Circuit Short To Ground or Open) C0034-62 Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Compare Failure Is DTC C0030-38, C0031-14, C0031-62, C0033-38, C0034-14, and/or C0034-62 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to the indicated VSA system DTC's troubleshooting . NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the left-front wheel speed sensor, the right-front wheel speed sensor, the VSA modulator-control unit, the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Clear the VSA system DTCs with the HDS.

Clear DTC (VSA)

-4. Start the engine.

- Vehicle speed above 19 mph (30 km/h) for more than 30 seconds

Values | Unit

Vehicle Speed

# DTC P0501 (K20C1) (17-19)

DTC P0501 : Output Shaft Speed Sensor Circuit Out of Range High

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0501 Output Shaft Speed Sensor Circuit Out of Range High

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit Vehicle Speed More than 327.6 km/h Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

Vehicle Speed | More than 327.6 | km/h

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor circuit connectors and terminals condition check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at these locations: Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor PCM Engine ground Body ground Are the connections and terminals OK? YES Go to step 3. NO Repair the connections or terminals.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

Check for poor connections or loose terminals at these locations:

- Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor

- PCM

- Engine ground

- Body ground

Are the connections and terminals OK?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Repair the connections or terminals.

- Shorted wire check (NC line to power) -1. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -2. Disconnect the following connectors. Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector PCM connector No. 1 (96P) -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector: disconnected PCM connector No. 1 (96P): disconnected Test point 1 Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there any voltage? YES Repair a short to power in the NC wire between PCM connector No. 1 terminal No. 38 and the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor. NO The NC wire is OK. Replace the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor .

-1. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

-2. Disconnect the following connectors.

Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector

PCM connector No. 1 (96P)

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle ON mode

Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector: disconnected

PCM connector No. 1 (96P): disconnected

Test point 1 | Output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2:

Test point 2 | Body ground

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there any voltage?

YES

Repair a short to power in the NC wire between PCM connector No. 1 terminal No. 38 and the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor.

NO

The NC wire is OK. Replace the output shaft (countershaft) speed sensor .
````

# DTC P0506 (K20C1) (17-18)

DTC P0506 : Idle Control System RPM Lower Than Expected

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0506 Idle Control System RPM Lower Than Expected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle at least 20 seconds. Check for these conditions in the DATA LIST with the HDS. Engine coolant temperature (ECT SENSOR 1) above 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) IAT SENSOR (1) above -23.4 deg.F (-30.8 deg.C) VEHICLE SPEED is 0 mph (0 km/h) AF FB (ST FUEL TRIM) between 0.75 and 1.25 FSS is CLOSED Signal Current conditions Values Unit ECT SENSOR 1 IAT SENSOR (1) VEHICLE SPEED AF FB (ST FUEL TRIM) FSS -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0506 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0506 Idle Control System RPM Lower Than Expected Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 3. If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, keep idling until a result comes on. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, go to step 1-5 and recheck.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle at least 20 seconds.

Check for these conditions in the DATA LIST with the HDS.

- Engine coolant temperature (ECT SENSOR 1) above 158 deg.F (70 deg.C)

- IAT SENSOR (1) above -23.4 deg.F (-30.8 deg.C)

- VEHICLE SPEED is 0 mph (0 km/h)

- AF FB (ST FUEL TRIM) between 0.75 and 1.25

- FSS is CLOSED

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

ECT SENSOR 1

IAT SENSOR (1)

VEHICLE SPEED

AF FB (ST FUEL TRIM)

FSS

-6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0506 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0506 Idle Control System RPM Lower Than Expected

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 3. If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, keep idling until a result comes on. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, go to step 1-5 and recheck.

- Throttle body visual check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the throttle body inlet pipe and the throttle body connector tube from the throttle body . -3. Check for dirt, carbon, or damage in the throttle bore. Is there dirt, carbon, or damage in the throttle bore? YES If there is dirt or carbon, clean the throttle body . Also check for damage to the air cleaner element. If there is damage in the throttle bore, replace the throttle body . NO The throttle body is OK. Check the A/C system, then go to step 4.

-2. Disconnect the throttle body inlet pipe and the throttle body connector tube from the throttle body .

-3. Check for dirt, carbon, or damage in the throttle bore.

YES

NO

- Throttle body visual check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the throttle body inlet pipe and the throttle body connector tube from the throttle body . -3. Also check for damage to the air cleaner element. If there is damage in the throttle bore, replace the throttle body . NO The throttle body is OK. Go to step 4.

-3. Check for dirt, carbon, or damage in the throttle bore.

YES

NO

# DTC P0507 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0507 : Idle Control System RPM Higher Than Expected

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0507 Idle Control System RPM Higher Than Expected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Let the engine cool to the ambient temperature. -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -4. Start the engine. -5. Let the engine idle without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. NOTE: Do not raise the engine speed by pressing the accelerator pedal. -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0507 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0507 Idle Control System RPM Higher Than Expected Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot. If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, keep idling until a result comes on. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, recheck with different load conditions (electrical, A/C, gear position, etc.), then go to step 1

-1. Let the engine cool to the ambient temperature.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-4. Start the engine.

-5. Let the engine idle without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

NOTE: Do not raise the engine speed by pressing the accelerator pedal.

-6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0507 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0507 Idle Control System RPM Higher Than Expected

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot. If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, keep idling until a result comes on. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, recheck with different load conditions (electrical, A/C, gear position, etc.), then go to step 1

- Vacuum leak check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Check for vacuum leaks at these parts: PCV valve PCV hose EVAP canister purge valve Throttle body Intake air duct Intake manifold All parts through the turbocharger to the throttle body Are there any leaks? YES Repair or replace the leaking part (s). NO Recheck with different load conditions (turn on the headlights, the blower motor, or the A/C, change the gear position, etc.).

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

Check for vacuum leaks at these parts:

- PCV valve

- PCV hose

- EVAP canister purge valve

- Throttle body

- Intake air duct

- Intake manifold

- All parts through the turbocharger to the throttle body

Are there any leaks?

YES

Repair or replace the leaking part (s).

NO

Recheck with different load conditions (turn on the headlights, the blower motor, or the A/C, change the gear position, etc.).
````

# DTC P050B (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY)

DTC P050B : Cold Start Ignition Timing Control System Performance Problem

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P050B Cold Start Ignition Timing Control System Performance Problem

DTC (PGM-FI)

- PGM-FI system check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC Are any Pending or Confirmed DTCs other than P050B indicated? YES Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

Are any Pending or Confirmed DTCs other than P050B indicated?

YES

Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Intake air duct visual check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check for poor connection or blockage at the intake air duct. Is it OK? YES Go to step 3. NO Reconnect or repair the intake air duct, or replace it if needed .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Check for poor connection or blockage at the intake air duct.

Is it OK?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Is it OK?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Replace the air cleaner housing .

- Air cleaner element visual check -1. Check for dirt or debris on the air cleaner element . Is it dirty? YES Replace the air cleaner element . NO Go to step 5.

-1. Check for dirt or debris on the air cleaner element .

Is it dirty?

YES

Replace the air cleaner element .

NO

Go to step 5.

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Let the engine cool until the value of ECT SENSOR 1 is 77 deg.F (25 deg.C) or less. Signal Current conditions Values Unit ECT SENSOR 1 -3. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -5. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds or more. -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P050B in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P050B Cold Start Ignition Timing Control System Performance Problem Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 6. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CKP sensor, the throttle body, MAF/IAT sensor 1, ECT sensor 1, ECT sensor 2, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, keep idling until a result comes on. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, go to step 5-2 and recheck.

P050B Cold Start Ignition Timing Control System Performance Problem

# DTC P0522 (K20C1) (17-18)

DTC P0522 : Rocker Arm Oil Pressure Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0522 Rocker Arm Oil Pressure Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR Less than 0 V Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the rocker arm oil pressure sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

YES

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the rocker arm oil pressure sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (OPSEN line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR Less than 0 V Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES Go to step 5. NO Go to step 3.

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Determine possible failure area (rocker arm oil pressure sensor, others) -1. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Test point 2 Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 5.0 V? YES Replace the rocker arm oil pressure sensor . NO Go to step 4.

-1. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle ON mode

Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Test point 2 | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 3:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there about 5.0 V?

YES

Replace the rocker arm oil pressure sensor .

NO

Go to step 4.

- Open wire check (VCC2 3 line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the following connector. PCM connector No. 1 (96P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector: disconnected PCM connector No. 1 (96P): disconnected Test point 1 Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Test point 2 PCM connector No. 1 (96P) No. 82 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES The VCC2 3 wire is OK. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC P0522 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . NO Repair an open in the VCC2 3 wire between PCM connector No. 1 terminal No. 82 and the rocker arm oil pressure sensor.

-2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

-4.

# DTC P0523 (K20C1) (17-18)

DTC P0523 : Rocker Arm Oil Pressure Sensor Circuit High Voltage

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0523 Rocker Arm Oil Pressure Sensor Circuit High Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR More than 5 V Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the rocker arm oil pressure sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR | More than 5 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the rocker arm oil pressure sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (rocker arm oil pressure sensor, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector -3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire. Terminal A Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Terminal B Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR More than 5 V Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES Go to step 3. NO Replace the rocker arm oil pressure sensor .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector

-3. Connect terminals A and B with a jumper wire.

Terminal A | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Terminal B | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 2:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

ROCKER ARM OIL PRESSURE SENSOR | More than 5 | V

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Replace the rocker arm oil pressure sensor .

- Determine possible failure area (SG5 line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Remove the jumper wire from the rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Test point 2 Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 3: Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 5.0 V? YES Go to step 5. NO Go to step 4.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Remove the jumper wire from the rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2.

Test condition | Vehicle ON mode

Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 1:

Test point 2 | Rocker arm oil pressure sensor 3P connector (female terminals) No. 3:

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there about 5.0 V?

YES

Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 4.

- Open wire check (SG5 line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the following connector. PCM connector No. 1 (96P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2.
````

# DTC P0532 (K20C1) (17-21)

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0532 A/C Pressure Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle. -4. Turn the blower switch on. -5. Turn the A/C switch on. -6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0532 A/C Pressure Sensor Circuit Low Voltage Is DTC P0532 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the A/C pressure sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle.

-4. Turn the blower switch on.

-5. Turn the A/C switch on.

-6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0532 A/C Pressure Sensor Circuit Low Voltage

Is DTC P0532 indicated?

YES

## Chunk 6799: DTC P053F (K20C1) (17-21)

- Title: DTC P053F (K20C1) (17-21)
- Source path: `pages\7557.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_07e7fb8701af`
